Implementation of objective_lse(x):
- Use _N, _NFFT.
- c = compute_c_all(x, N)
- m=max(c); s=sum(exp(alpha(c-m))); return (m + log(s)/alpha)* (2.0/_N)
For alpha maybe large. If alpha <=0? Use exact max? Not differentiable. We'll always use α.

jac_lse(x):
- compute c_all.
- stable weights:
  m = c.max()
  a = _ALPHA
  sh = a*(c-m)
  maxsh=sh.max() (0 maybe)
  p = np.exp(sh - maxsh)  # if maxsh not 0? Actually sh max 0 unless m from c; due numeric sh≤0. Use p.
  S = p.sum()
  w = p / (a * S)  # length 2n-1
- extract w0,w_neg,w_pos
- b = np.full(n,w0)+pos_contrib+neg_contrib
- K = np.zeros(N,N?); K[0]=2*w0; if n>1: d=np.arange(1,n); K[d]=S_w; K[N-d]=S_w (N-d values >n? okay as long N>2d; N>=2n, yes N-d >= n? for d<=n-1, N-d >=2n-(n-1)=n+1, distinct)
- H = np.zeros(N); H[:n]=x
- y = irfft(rfft(K,N)*conj(rfft(H,N)),N).real[(N - np.arange(n)) % N]
- grad = (b - y) * (2.0/n)
Return.

Potential numerical: K construction each jac O(n). Good.

SLSQP with equality constraint sum - n/2 =0 and bounds. Constraint function sum(x)-target, jac ones. But SLSQP might be slow; maxiter 2000, ftol 1e-12. It uses gradient. However equality constraint can be handled; SLSQP may be okay for n=250. Need set options? SLSQP doesn't accept cf? It does. Use constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':sum_constraint,'jac':ones_jac}] but no lambdas; top-level functions sum_func, sum_jac using global _Target.

But SLSQP with many iterations computing FFT each time; each SLSQP iteration may call objective multiple times and gradient once? Good. n=250, maxiter 2000 maybe too slow? Each eval ~2 FFTs length 512/1024, microseconds. SLSQP line search maybe many. Could be fine under 1100s. But SLSQP may struggle with large n=500; we can use lower n.

Alternative: Use custom L-BFGS projected. But SLSQP is simpler to enforce equality and bounds. We can run from multiple α and starts.

Global manager:
```
_N = 0
_ALPHA = 1.0
_NFFT = 0
_TARGET = 0.0

def set_state(n, alpha):
    global _N,_ALPHA,_NFFT,_TARGET
    _N=n; _ALPHA=float(alpha); _TARGET=n/2.0
    N=1
    while N < 2*n: N <<=1
    _NFFT=N
```

Compute c_all raw:
```
def fast_c_all(h, n, N):
    H = np.zeros(N); H[:n]=h
    F = np.fft.rfft(H, N)
    R = np.fft.irfft(F * np.conj(F), N).real
    P = R[:n]
    prefix = np.empty(n+1); prefix[0]=0; prefix[1:]=np.cumsum(h)
    total = prefix[n]
    pos = total - prefix[:n] - P
    neg = prefix[n:0:-1] - P
    c = np.empty(2*n-1)
    c[:n] = neg[1:][::-1] # if n>1
    c[n] = pos[0]
    c[n+1:] = pos[1:]
    return c
```
Need handle n=1? not relevant, but can. If n=1, c length1. But n even.

But FFT autocorr for P: If H real, F*conj(F) maybe small imaginary in irfft? real. P[d] may have tiny negative. Good.

Maybe P[0] includes h^2. c0=total-P0 = ∑h_i(1-h_i) (raw); times dx. If h binary c0=0 due exact? With numerical maybe small. Good.

Objective:
```
def lse_objective(x):
   n=_N; c=fast_c_all(x,n,_NFFT); m=c.max(); sh=_ALPHA*(c-m);
   # if all -inf? no
   maxsh=sh.max(); p=np.exp(sh-maxsh); S=p.sum()
   return (m + (np.log(S)+maxsh)/_ALPHA) * (2.0/n)
```

def slsqp_improve(h, alpha, maxiter, time_limit):
   global _N,_ALPHA,_NFFT,_TARGET
   n=len(h); set_state(n,alpha)
   h0=project_feasible(h,n)
   res=minimize(lse_objective,h0,method='SLSQP',bounds=[(0,1)]*n,
      constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':sum_func,'jac':sum_jac}],
      jac=lse_gradient, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12})
   h=res.x
   h=project_feasible(h,n)
   return h, true_c(h,n)
```

Projection:
```
def project_feasible(h, n):
    target = n/2.0
    h = np.clip(np.asarray(h,float),0,1)
    # waterfilling via binary search
    # sum as function of tau: clip(h - tau,0,1). Need target.
    lo = h.min() - 1.0
    hi = h.max() + 1.0
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       s=np.clip(h-mid,0,1).sum()
       if s > target: hi=mid
       else: lo=mid
    tau=(lo+hi)/2
    hp=np.clip(h-tau,0,1)
    # correct tiny residual by adjusting free interior
    diff=target-hp.sum()
    if abs(diff)>0:
       free=(hp>1e-12)&(hp<1-1e-12)
       if free.any(): hp[free] += diff/free.sum()
       else:
           # fallback linear adjustments
    return np.clip(hp,0,1)
```
Wait projection of y onto sum target with box uses h=clip(y - tau). If sum > target, need increase tau (subtract more); if sum<target, decrease tau. Binary: if s>target hi=mid (tau too low? large tau reduces sum, so if s>target, need bigger tau => hi=mid? Let's define tau; s decreases as tau increases. If s>target, current tau too small; set lo=mid? If tau low, sum high. Need increase tau: lo=mid. If s<target, tau too high: hi=mid. So:
```
if s > target: lo = mid
else: hi = mid
```

Augmented Lagrangian:
- Keep multiplier ν. Minimize Fα(h) + ν(sum-target) + (ρ/2)(sum-target)^2.

Augmented L-BFGS-B top-level:
Globals: _ALPHA, _N, _NFFT, _MU (multiplier), _RHO (penalty).
```
def aug_obj(x):
    f = lse_objective(x)
    e = x.sum() - _TARGET
    return f + _MU*e + 0.5*_RHO*e*e
def aug_jac(x):
    g = lse_gradient(x)
    e = x.sum() - _TARGET
    return g + (_MU + _RHO*e)*np.ones(_N)
```
Use `minimize(aug_obj, h, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, jac=aug_jac, options={'maxiter':..., 'ftol':1e-12, 'maxls':20})`.

Full local search: We can do greedy pair swaps: For sweeps, for each i in ones, for each j in zeros, try swap if potentially improve. But doing all pairs every sweep maybe okay. We can optimize by skipping if newmax >= best. To find best move, maybe one pass. Code:
```
def binary_local_search(h, n, deadline):
  target=n//2
  idx= np.argsort(h)[::-1] (h continuous); hb=zeros; hb[idx[:target]]=1.
  c=fast_c_all(hb,n,N); c[center]=0
  best=max(c); ones=zset.
  for sweep in range(max_sweeps):
    improved=False
    # To limit time, choose random subset if deadline
    for i in ones_list:
      for j in zeros_list:
         tmp=c.copy(); apply_flip(tmp,hb,i,-1); apply_flip(tmp,hb,j,1); nb=tmp.max(); if nb<best - 1e-12: accept: c=tmp; best=nb; update lists; improved=True; break loops
    if not improved: break
  return hb, best*dx
```

Let's write apply_flip formula fully:
```
def apply_flip(c, h, p, delta):
    n = len(h)
    center = n-1
    # neighbors product
    prod = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if p > 0:
        # d=1..p corresponds q=p-d = 0..p-1 reversed
        prod[1:p+1] += h[0:p][::-1]
    if p < n-1:
        prod[1:n-p] += h[p+1:n]
    # multiply by -delta
    prod[1:] *= -delta
    # positive lag indices center+1..2n-2
    c[center+1:] += prod[1:]
    # negative lags: c[0]=center-(n-1) corresponds d=n-1, ... c[center-1]=d=1.
    c[:center][::-1] += prod[1:]
    # linear positive: h_p as second variable for d<=p, d=1..min(p,n-1)
    qpos = min(p, n-1)
    if qpos > 0:
        c[center+1:center+1+qpos] += delta
    # linear negative: h_p as first variable for d<=n-1-p
    qneg = n-1-p
    if qneg > 0:
        c[:center][::-1][:qneg] += delta
    # c0 d=0? if binary c0 unchanged; but for general? We can leave. For binary, if h becomes 0/1, c0=0.
    h[p] += delta
```
But check product add to negative: `c[:center][::-1]` is a view with non-positive strides; adding prod modifies correct elements. Good.
Linear negative using same view first qneg elements (d=1..qneg) okay.
Potential double count if applying sequential: linear changes accumulate. For swap with sum fixed, c0 should be unchanged. For individual flips not integer? But we only binary.

Correlate h,1-h: v=[0,1]. full correlate? [0,?]. np.correlate([1,0],[0,1]) = [0,1,0]? max1. yes.

I wrote `prod[1:n-p]`: n-p=2, slice 1:2 length1 -> add h[1]=0. prod = [0,0]. Add to pos d=1: c[2] += -1; neg d=1 c[0]+=-1. qpos=min(1,1)=1: c[2] += +1 -> net0; qneg=0. Original h [1,0] c [1,0,0]. New h [1,1]? sum not target; c should? v=[0,0], correlate zeros -> all0. Our c[0]=0,c[2]=0 good. c0 left0 but new h both1, c0=∑h∑h^2=2-2=0. Good.
Flip p=1 1->0 delta=-1 from [1,1] to [1,0]: neighbors left h0=1 prod[1]+=1 * -(-1)? -delta = +1? Delta=-1 -> prod[1]=1. c pos +=1, neg +=1; qpos=1 cpos += -1 net0; qneg=0. c [0,0,0] -> [1,0,0]. Good.

Let's define `next_power_of_two_2n(n)` top-level:
```
def nfft_for(n):
    N=1
    need=2*n
    while N<need: N*=2
    return N
```

```

Now, L-BFGS-B augmented functions. Need set globals `_MU`, `_RHO`.
```
def set_ll_state(n, alpha, mu, rho): set_state; _MU=mu; _RHO=rho
def ll_obj(x):
   f=lse_objective(x)
   e=x.sum()-_TARGET
   return f + _MU*e + 0.5*_RHO*e*e
def ll_jac(x):
   g=lse_gradient(x)
   e=x.sum()-_TARGET
   return g + (_MU + _RHO*e)*np.ones(_N)
```
Using `np.ones(_N)` each call; can global `_ONEN`? Top-level helper. But n changes; create. Could return `g +  ...` (broadcast scalar) no np.ones needed! Since g array, adding scalar broadcasts. `return g + (_MU + _RHO*e)`? This adds scalar to each component, yes gradient of penalty scalar sum is constant vector; adding scalar to array works. But adding scalar to array modifies? Returns new. So `g + (_MU + _RHO*e)` works. Need ensure not integer? float.

But `lse_gradient` returns dx-scaled raw grad. Penalty scaled in same objective C units. Good.

In `optimize_lbfgsb`, we need update best across calls. It can return best_h,best_c,mu? For simplicity:
```
def optimize_lbfgsb(h,n,deadline,alphas,maxiter_base,mu=0.0,rho=20.0):
    best_h = project_feasible(h,n)
    best_c = fast_max_c(best_h,n)
    target = n/2.0
    bounds = [(0.0,1.0)]*n
    for alpha in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        # maybe two rho levels
        for rho_use in [rho, rho*10]:
            if time.time() > deadline: break
            set_ll_state(n,alpha,mu,rho_use)
            try:
                res = minimize(ll_objective, best_h, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, jac=ll_gradient, options={'maxiter':maxiter_base,'ftol':1e-12,'maxls':20,'disp':False})
            except Exception:
                res = None
            if res is not None:
                h2 = project_feasible(res.x,n)
                c2 = fast_max_c(h2,n)
                if c2 < best_c - 1e-15:
                    best_c = c2; best_h = h2
            else: h2=best_h; c2=best_c
            e = h2.sum() - target
            # update mu based on last feasible? If using L-BFGS result before projection, maybe res.x sum.
            if res is not None:
                e = float(res.x.sum() - target)
            mu += rho_use * e
            if abs(e) > 1e-5:
                rho = rho*2
            else:
                rho = max(1.0, rho/2)
            h2=project_feasible(h2,n)
    return best_h,best_c
```

This formula corresponds to C[k]=∑ H_n K_{n+k}? We derived y_i=C[-i]. Let's double-check with DFT for real. Let Kf_l = ∑_m K_m e^{-2πi ml/N}; Hf_l = ∑_n H_n e^{-2πi nl/N}. conj(Hf_l)=∑_n H_n e^{+2πi nl/N}. Product =∑_{m,n}K_m H_n e^{-2πi l(m-n)/N}. irfft k: (1/N)∑_l product e^{2πi kl/N}=∑ K_m H_n δ_{k,m-n mod N} =∑_m K_m H_{m-k mod N}. Let n = m-k -> m=n+k. C[k]=∑_n H_n K_{n+k}. We want y_i=∑_m K_m H_{i+m}; let n = i+m -> m=n-i. Also K_m; y_i=∑_n H_n K_{n-i}. That's C[k] with k=-i mod N: C[-i]=∑_n H_n K_{n-i}. Yes. So y_i as C[-i]. Our index `idx = (N - np.arange(n)) % N`, for i=0 0, i=1 N-1, etc. Good.

Projection binary search detail:
```
def project_feasible(h, n=None):
    if n is None: n=len(h)
    target=n/2.0
    h=np.clip(np.asarray(h,dtype=float),0.0,1.0)
    # if sum already close, return but adjust?
    lo = h.min() - 1.0
    hi = h.max() + 1.0
    for _ in range(70):
       mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
       s=np.clip(h-mid,0.0,1.0).sum()
       if s > target:
          lo = mid
       else:
          hi = mid
    tau=(lo+hi)*0.5
    hp=np.clip(h-tau,0.0,1.0)
    # refine with free adjustment
    for _ in range(10):
       diff=target-hp.sum()
       if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
       free=(hp>1e-12) & (hp<1.0-1e-12)
       if free.any():
          hp[free] += diff / free.sum()
       else:
          # if target not at boundary, add tiny to zeros/ones? But target n/2, should free.
          # fallback distribute uniformly and clip
          hp = np.clip(hp + diff/n,0,1)
    return np.clip(hp,0,1)
```
The binary search direction: tau high -> sum low. If s > target, need higher tau, so lo=mid. If s < target, hi=mid. Yes. Initial lo = min(h)-1 -> mid small, clip(h-mid)=1 mostly sum n >target, lo increases. hi=max+1 sum 0. Good.

But if target n/2 and h in [0,1], feasible; binary 70 enough to precision ~range/2^70 ~1e-20. Good.
After free adjustment, adding diff/free.sum might push some outside if diff large; loop clips. If no free (all 0/1) and sum not target, fallback may not exact. But for binary with sum target, free maybe zeros. For interpolation, should have free.

def fast_max_c(h):
    n=len(h)
    N=nfft_for(n)
    c=compute_c_all(h,n,N)
    return float(np.max(c) * (2.0/n))
```

`exact_c`:
```
def exact_c(h,n):
    c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
    return float(np.max(c)*(2.0/n))
```

def make_binary(h,n):

Binary local search implementation details:
```
def binary_local_search(h_cont, n, deadline, max_sweeps=20):
    if n %2 !=0: return h_cont, inf
    target=n//2
    hb = np.zeros(n)
    order = np.argsort(h_cont)[::-1]
    hb[order[:target]]=1.0
    N=nfft_for(n)
    c=compute_c_all(hb,n,N)
    center=n-1
    c[center]=0.0
    best_c = float(c.max())
    best_h=hb.copy()
    ones = np.where(hb==1.0)[0].tolist()
    zeros = np.where(hb==0.0)[0].tolist()
    cdx=2.0/n
    best_bound=best_c*cdx
    for sweep in range(max_sweeps):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       improved=False
       # maybe shuffle starts? Use rng? No rng in function? Could use global np.random? top-level no. We can accept deterministic.
       for i in ones:
          if time.time()>deadline: break
          # optional skip if? 
          for j in zeros:
             if time.time()>deadline: break
             # if j==i no
             # apply
             tmp = c.copy()
             # current h should have i=1,j=0
             # first flip i to 0
             apply_binary_flip(tmp, hb, i, -1.0)
             # now hb[i]=0; flip j to 1
             apply_binary_flip(tmp, hb, j, 1.0)
             nb = float(tmp.max())
             # revert h
             hb[i]=1.0; hb[j]=0.0
             if nb + 1e-12 < best_c:
                c = tmp
                hb[i]=0.0; hb[j]=1.0
                best_c=nb
                best_h=hb.copy()
                # update lists: remove j from zeros, remove i? i no longer one; add j? ones gets j, zeros gets i
                # Easiest rebuild:
                ones = [idx for idx in range(n) if hb[idx]==1.0] # O(n)
                zeros = [idx for idx in range(n) if hb[idx]==0.0]
                improved=True
                break
          if improved:
             break
       if not improved: break
    return hb, best_c*cdx
```
But after evaluating and not accepting, we set hb[i]=1,hb[j]=0. In apply first flip, hb i becomes0, second j becomes1. Revert. Good. If accepted, after apply hb i=0,j=1; set best_h. Then break. Rebuild lists. Note if we break out of inner after accept, no need revert. Good.

Potential performance: Rebuild lists using list comprehensions Python O(n) per accept, okay. But full pairwise loop over `for i in ones` where ones list changes? We rebuild only after accept and break sweep. Next sweep uses updated. Good.

But in inner loop, `for j in zeros`: if we check current h but after not accepting we revert, h consistent. Good.

Can improve by precomputing zeros list; if i in ones and j in zeros. Accept break from both; okay.

Potential bug: apply_binary_flip uses `h` for neighbors. For first flip, if j adjacent and currently 0, product change correct. After first flip, second uses i=0 correct. Good.

Random Fourier start function top-level:
```
def random_smooth_start(n, rng):
    h = np.full(n,0.5)
    for k in range(1,6):
       amp = rng.uniform(0.05,0.2)/math.sqrt(k)
       phi = rng.uniform(0,2*math.pi)
       idx = np.arange(n)
       h += amp*np.sin(2*math.pi*k*idx/n + phi)
    return project_feasible(h,n)
```

```
def lbfgsb_step(h,n,alpha,maxiter,rho):
    set_ll_state(n,alpha,0.0,rho)
    bounds = [(0.0,1.0)]*n
    options = {'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'ftol': 1e-12, 'maxls': 20, 'disp': False}
    try:
        res = minimize(ll_objective, h, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, jac=ll_gradient, options=options)
    except Exception:
        return h
    return project_feasible(res.x, n)
```

def optimize_lbfgsb(h,n,deadline,alphas,maxiter_base,rho_base=20.0):
    best_h=project_feasible(h,n)
    best_c=fast_max_c(best_h)
    target=n/2.0
    # alphas list values
    for alpha in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        rho = rho_base
        for rep in range(2):
            if time.time() > deadline: break
            h = lbfgsb_step(best_h,n,alpha,maxiter_base,rho)
            c = fast_max_c(h)
            if c < best_c:
               best_c=c; best_h=h.copy()
            # estimate residual from unprojected? we lost res. Could recompute h_sum before project? Not accessible. Use projected sum zero. So cannot know residual. Projected sum exact, so residual always zero; rho won't increase. That's okay. Maybe use rho fixed.
        # optionally if c improvement small, increase rho? not.
    return best_h,best_c
```

def optimize_slsqp(h,n,deadline,alphas,maxiter):
    best_h=project_feasible(h,n); best_c=fast_max_c(best_h)
    for alpha in alphas:
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       h,c=slsqp_step(best_h,n,alpha,maxiter)
       if c<best_c: update
    return best_h,best_c

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+budget_s-6.0
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    # parent
    try:
       par = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
       par = np.full(250,0.5)
    if par.size < 10:
       par = np.full(250,0.5)
    n_par = par.size
    # if odd? keep for LSE, binary skip.
    h_best = project_feasible(par, n_par)
    c_best = fast_max_c(h_best)
    n_best = n_par
    # sym version
    h_sym = project_feasible(0.5*(h_best + h_best[::-1]), n_best)
    c_sym = fast_max_c(h_sym)
    if c_sym < c_best: update
    # Phase core
    total = budget_s - 5.0
    core_end = t0 + 0.42*total
    # L-BFGS on best n
    alphas_parent = [5,10,20,50,100,200,500,1000,2000]
    maxiter_parent = 250 if n_best<=300 else 150
    h_best,c_best = optimize_lbfgsb(h_best,n_best,core_end, alphas_parent, maxiter_parent)
    # SLSQP parent if n<=300 and time
    if n_best <= 300 and time.time() < core_end - 80:
       h_sl,c_sl = optimize_slsqp(h_best,n_best,core_end, [200,500,1000,2000], 150)
       if c_sl < c_best: update
    # Random/sym starts maybe within core if time
    for _ in range(2):
       if time.time() > core_end - 40: break
       h_rand = project_feasible(h_best + 0.08*rng.randn(n_best), n_best)
       # optimize lbfgsb small
       h_r,c_r = optimize_lbfgsb(h_rand,n_best,min(deadline? core_end?), [50,200,500], 120)
       update
    # Other resolutions

    cands=[]

    # exclude already parent? can include once.

       h2,c2 = optimize_lbfgsb(h,nn, min(res_end, ???), alphas_nn, maxit)
       update
       if nn<=300 and time.time()<res_end-60:

          update

def resample_h(h, n):

    if m==n: return h

Could there be an issue with using `linspace(0,2,n)` endpoints? The discrete representation as n samples over [0,2] with dx=2/n: Are samples [0,2) or inclusive? They say n_points samples over [0,2], dx=2/n. Usually cell centers? The evaluation uses sum*dx, so effectively n cells of width dx, not endpoints inclusive. `np.linspace(0,2,n)` includes both endpoints with spacing 2/(n-1), but for interpolation from parent, using [0,2] endpoints with n points may not correspond to cells. However the objective does not depend on positions, only order and length. Interpolating from one discretization to another: The samples represent cells maybe at centers (i+0.5)*dx. If we use interpolation over [0,2] with endpoints inclusive, we may slightly shift boundary. It may not matter for local optimization; but resampling from parent n=250 to n needs consistent coordinate. We can use cell centers: x0=(np.arange(m)+0.5)*2/m, x1=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2/n. That likely matches Riemann sum. Use `np.linspace`? The problem says over [0,2] with dx=2/n; no explicit centers. Since integration sum*dx, samples likely cell centers or grid points [0,2-dx]. Using centers is safest. Interpolation endpoints not critical. Let's implement cell centers.
```
def resample_h(h,n):
   m=len(h)
   if m==n: return h
   x0=(np.arange(m)+0.5)*(2.0/m)
   x1=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*(2.0/n)
   return np.interp(x1,x0,h)
```
Good.

Symmetry reversal with cell centers: For even n, reverse maps cell i to n-1-i; centers symmetric? center_i=(i+.5)*2/n, center_{n-1-i}=(n-1-i+.5)=n-i-.5; sum 2? yes. Good. For odd also.

Top-level `ll_objective(x)`:
```
def ll_objective(x):
    f = lse_objective(x)
    e = x.sum() - _TARGET
    return f + 0.5 * _RHO * e * e
```
`ll_gradient`:
```
def ll_gradient(x):
    g = lse_gradient(x)
    e = x.sum() - _TARGET
    return g + _RHO * e
```
Adding scalar to array returns new. Good. Since gradient of e is ones; scalar adds to all components.

Potential issue: If `_RHO` is integer, scalar addition ok. If e large, gradient large.

`slsqp_step` uses equality constraint; no penalty. Need `constraint_jac` top-level:
```
def constraint_jac(x):
    return np.ones_like(x, dtype=float)
```
`constraint_fun(x): return float(x.sum() - _TARGET)`.

In `slsqp_step`:
```
def slsqp_step(h,n,alpha,maxiter):
    set_lse_state(n,alpha)
    h0=project_feasible(h,n)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':constraint_fun,'jac':constraint_jac}]
    options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-12,'disp':False}
    try:
       res=minimize(lse_objective,h0,method='SLSQP',bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,jac=lse_gradient,options=options)
       h1=project_feasible(res.x,n)
    except Exception:
       return h0, fast_max_c(h0)
    return h1, fast_max_c(h1)
```
SLSQP may return x if success. Good.

`lbfgsb_step` uses bounds and `ll_objective`. It should not set state? We'll set before.
```
def lbfgsb_step(h,n,alpha,maxiter,rho):
    set_ll_state(n,alpha,rho)
    h0=project_feasible(h,n)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-12,'maxls':20,'disp':False}
    try:
       res=minimize(ll_objective,h0,method='L-BFGS-B',bounds=bounds,jac=ll_gradient,options=options)
       h1=project_feasible(res.x,n)
    except Exception:
       return h0
    return h1
```
Could use `gtol` maybe. No.

Now, consider using **irreducible objective** exact vs fast in binary: Fast c updated by flips may drift due floating. We set c0=0, apply incremental; after many accepted moves, numerical errors could accumulate (integers but float). To sanitize occasionally, recompute c from h using FFT. In binary search, after each sweep or every few accepts, recompute `c=compute_c_all(hb,n,N); c[center]=0; best_c=fast`. Since values integers small, errors tiny. Recompute at start of each sweep to avoid drift:
```
for sweep:
   c = compute_c_all(hb,n,N); c[center]=0; best_c=round? float(max(c))
```
This O(n logn) per sweep negligible. Then local swaps. After accept, c remains consistent; recompute next sweep. Good.

`apply_binary_flip` produces floats; max may be 0.999999999 due additions. Use `int`? We can round c to nearest maybe:
```
c = np.rint(c)  # after recompute? FFT gives exact? Use rb = np.rint(c) for max. But in updates, maybe.```
But c values are sums of products and linear; for binary all integers. FFT may give 0.999999999. We can round initial c: `c = np.rint(compute_c_all(...)); c[center]=0`. That gives exact integers. For updated c, after each flip, if we want exact, we could also round after operations: `tmp = np.rint(tmp)`? But if we round inside, linear+product exact integers. apply_flip modifies by delta (int) and prod values 0/1; result exact integer mathematically but float; rounding after each apply maybe okay but extra. Could just round when checking max: `nb_guess = float(tmp.max())`; if near, okay. Use tolerance 1e-9. Since c expected integers, use `nb_rounded = float(np.rint(tmp).max())` for comparison; and if accepted, set c = np.rint(tmp) to keep integers. That's extra O(n) but okay. Let's do in binary local:
After two flips, `tmp = np.rint(tmp)`; `nb = float(tmp.max())`. If accept, `c = tmp`. This ensures integer consistency. Recompute at each sweep and rint. Good.

       # line search factors

But gradient_from_weights must handle arbitrary weights; similar to lse_gradient weight extraction but no α normalization (weights sum maybe1). We can implement `raw_gradient_weights(h, weights, n, N)` and multiply dx. This uses K0=2*w0, etc. Boundary b from weights. Product K S. Return b-y.
```
def gradient_from_weights(h, weights, n, N):
   center=n-1
   w0=weights[center]
   b=np.full(n,w0)
   if n>1:
      w_neg=weights[:center][::-1]
      w_pos=weights[center+1:]
      cp=np.cumsum(w_pos); b[1:]+=cp
      cn=np.cumsum(w_neg); b[:n-1]+=cn[::-1]
      S=w_pos+w_neg
      K=np.zeros(N); H=np.zeros(N); H[:n]=h
      K[0]=2*w0
      d=np.arange(1,n)
      K[d]+=S; K[N-d]+=S
      y=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(K,N)*np.conj(np.fft.rfft(H,N)),N).real
      prod=y[(N-np.arange(n))%N]
   else:
      prod=2*w0*h
   return b-prod
```
Then lse_gradient can call this with weights and multiply dx? To avoid duplication, `lse_gradient` computes w and uses `gradient_from_weights` and returns dx*raw. But `gradient_from_weights` would allocate K/H; lse_gradient also needs c? We can refactor:
- `compute_c_all`
- `raw_gradient_from_weights(h, weights, n, N)`
- `lse_gradient`: compute c, w, return (2/n)*raw_gradient_from_weights(h,w,n,N)
- active polish uses raw_gradient_from_weights.

Let's implement `raw_gradient_from_weights`:
```
def raw_gradient_from_weights(h, weights, n, N):
    center=n-1
    w0=float(weights[center])
    b=np.full(n,w0)
    if n>1:
       w_neg=weights[:center][::-1]
       w_pos=weights[center+1:]
       cp=np.cumsum(w_pos)
       b[1:1+len(cp)] += cp
       cn=np.cumsum(w_neg)
       b[:n-1] += cn[::-1]
       S=w_pos + w_neg
       K=np.zeros(N)
       H=np.zeros(N); H[:n]=h
       K[0]=2.0*w0
       if n>1:
          d=np.arange(1,n)
          K[d] += S
          K[N-d] += S
       y=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(K,N) * np.conj(np.fft.rfft(H,N)), N).real
       idx=(N - np.arange(n)) % N
       prod=y[idx]
    else:
       prod=2.0*w0*h
    return b - prod
```
For n=1, weights length1. Not needed.

This duplicates K/H allocation; for lse_gradient, okay. Could precompute d,N? Not.

def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kwargs):
    t0=time.time()
    deadline=t0+budget_s-6.0
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try: parent=np.asarray(initial_h_values,dtype=float)
    except: parent=np.full(250,0.5)
    if parent.size<20: parent=np.full(250,0.5)
    # Ensure parent not modify? project creates new.
    h_best=project_feasible(parent,parent.size)
    n_best=h_best.size
    c_best=fast_max_c(h_best)
    # symmetric improvement
    hs=project_feasible(0.5*(h_best+h_best[::-1]),n_best)
    cs=fast_max_c(hs)
    if cs<c_best: h_best=hs;c_best=cs
    total=budget_s-5.0
    core_end=t0+0.40*total
    # Parent L-BFGS
    if n_best <= 400:
       alphas=[5,10,20,50,100,200,500,1000,2000]
    else:
       alphas=[10,50,200,500]
    maxit=250 if n_best<=300 else 120
    h2,c2=optimize_lbfgsb(h_best,n_best,core_end,alphas,maxit)
    if c2<c_best: update
    # SLSQP parent if n<=300
    if n_best<=300 and time.time() < core_end - 120:
       h3,c3=optimize_slsqp(h_best,n_best,core_end,[200,500,1000,2000],150)
       if c3<c_best: update
    # noise starts within core if time
    if time.time() < core_end - 60:
       h_no=project_feasible(h_best+0.1*rng.randn(n_best),n_best)
       h4,c4=optimize_lbfgsb(h_no,n_best,core_end,[50,200,500],100)
       if c4<c_best: update
    # Other resolutions
    res_end=t0+0.75*total
    candidates=[]
    # include parent? skip if already done.
    for nn in [200,250,300,350,400,500,600,750]:
       if nn%2==0 and abs(nn-n_best)>1 and nn>50:
          candidates.append(nn)
    # prioritize near parent? maybe sort by abs(nn-n_best)
    candidates.sort(key=lambda x: abs(x-n_best)) # lambda not allowed? "no lambdas"? In run, lambdas prohibited? It says no closures or lambdas. Avoid lambda. Use custom function `sort_by_abs`?     for nn in fixed_list:
       if nn==n_best or odd or nn<100: continue
       if time.time() > res_end - 50: break
       h = resample_h(h_best, nn)
       hs = project_feasible(0.5*(h+h[::-1]), nn)
       # choose start lower
       if fast_max_c(hs) < fast_max_c(h): h=hs
       # if parent n maybe > nn? okay.
       if nn <= 300:
          alphas=[10,50,200,500,1000]; maxit=150
       elif nn <= 500:
          alphas=[20,100,300,1000]; maxit=100
       else:
          alphas=[20,100,300]; maxit=60
       h_new,c_new=optimize_lbfgsb(h, nn, res_end, alphas, maxit)
       if c_new < c_best: update
       if nn <= 300 and time.time() < res_end - 70:
          h_sl,c_sl=optimize_slsqp(h_new, nn, res_end, [500,1000], 100)
          if c_sl < c_new: update
    # final active polish on best n if time
    if time.time() < deadline - 60:
       h_act,c_act=active_max_polish(h_best,n_best,deadline-20, max_iter=3000)
       if c_act < c_best: update
    # binary local search on best n (if <=350) and perhaps 250
    bin_n = n_best if n_best %2==0 and n_best<=350 else 250

    # final project/exact

    return h_best, float(c_best), int(n_best)

Let's implement active_max_polish carefully:
```
def active_max_polish(h,n,deadline,max_iter=3000):
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=fast_max_c(h); N=nfft_for(n); dx=2.0/n; lr=0.05
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time.time() > deadline: break
       c=compute_c_all(h,n,N)
       maxc=float(c.max()); curr=maxc*dx
       if curr < best_c: best_c=curr; best_h=h.copy()
       tol=1e-7
       active=np.where(c >= maxc - tol)[0]
       if active.size == 0:
           active=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
       elif active.size > 500:
           # take top 500 by c (since active sorted by index, not top)
           idxs=np.argsort(c)[::-1][:500]
           active=idxs
       w=np.zeros(2*n-1)
       w[active]=1.0/active.size
       g=dx * raw_gradient_from_weights(h, w, n, N)
       g = g - g.mean()
       ng=float(np.linalg.norm(g))
       if ng < 1e-14: break
       gn = g / ng
       found=False
       # step size factors: start with lr/ ng? We normalized g, use step = lr*factor
       for factor in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005]:
           step=lr*factor
           ht=project_feasible(h - step*gn, n)
           ct=fast_max_c(ht)
           if ct < curr - 1e-13:
               h=ht; curr=ct
               if curr < best_c:
                   best_c=curr; best_h=h.copy()
               found=True
               break
       if found:
           lr = min(lr*1.05, 1.0)
       else:
           lr *= 0.8
           if lr < 1e-10: break
    return best_h, best_c
```
But if `curr` maybe not best because h improved? We update h only if accept; we don't update h to ht if ct not less current but less best? It can't be less best without less current because best≤current? Actually best_c <= curr (best historical). If ct < best_c then also < curr. So fine.

Subgradient direction: `h - step*gn`; if `gn` already normalized from g. If g small, step. Good.

Tolerance active 1e-7 raw (~8e-10 C for n=250). Might exclude active due numerical; if too few, uniform top. Could be okay. Maybe use 1e-5 raw to include near active. If continuous plateau, many active. Use `tol = max(1e-6, 1e-9 * maxc)`? maxc~50 -> 5e-8. 1e-6 raw =8e-9 C. Very tight. Maybe 1e-4 raw =8e-7 C. Use 1e-5 raw =8e-8. Good. Active set maybe few. Use `tol=1e-5`.

def nfft_for(n):
    N=1
    need=2*n+1  # enough? Need N >= 2n-1 for autocorr and 2n? For K negative no overlap need N>=2n-1. Use 2*n to be safe.
    while N < need:
        N <<= 1
    return N
```

def nfft_for(n):
    N=1
    need=2*n
    if need < 2: need=2
    while N < need: N <<=1
    return N

`compute_c_all` if N maybe nfft_for.
```
def compute_c_all(h,n,N):
    if n == 1:
       H=np.zeros(N); H[0]=h[0]
       F=np.fft.rfft(H,N); P0=float(np.fft.irfft(F*np.conj(F),N).real[0])
       total=float(h[0]); return np.array([total-P0])
    H=np.zeros(N); H[:n]=h
    F=np.fft.rfft(H,N)
    R=np.fft.irfft(F*np.conj(F),N).real
    P=R[:n]
    prefix=np.empty(n+1); prefix[0]=0.0; prefix[1:]=np.cumsum(h)
    total=prefix[n]
    pos = total - prefix[:n] - P
    neg = prefix[n:0:-1] - P
    c=np.empty(2*n-1, dtype=float)
    center=n-1
    c[:center] = neg[1:][::-1]
    c[center] = pos[0]
    c[center+1:] = pos[1:]
    return c
```
If h is list, `H[:n]=h` works if h list length n. `np.cumsum(h)` works.

`fast_max_c(h)`:
```
def fast_max_c(h):
    n=len(h)
    N=nfft_for(n)
    c=compute_c_all(h,n,N)
    val=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(val): return 1e100
    return val * (2.0/n)
```

def set_lse_state(n, alpha):
    global _N,_ALPHA,_NFFT,_TARGET
    _N=int(n); _ALPHA=float(alpha); _TARGET=float(_N)/2.0; _NFFT=nfft_for(_N)
```

`lse_objective`:
```
def lse_objective(x):
    n=_N; c=compute_c_all(x,n,_NFFT)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    a=_ALPHA
    sh=a*(c-m)
    maxsh=float(np.max(sh))
    p=np.exp(sh-maxsh)
    S=float(p.sum())
    if S <=0 or not np.isfinite(S): return 1e100
    val = (m + (maxsh + np.log(S))/a) * (2.0/n)
    return val if np.isfinite(val) else 1e100
```
If a very small? no.

`raw_gradient_from_weights`, and `lse_gradient`:
```
def raw_gradient_from_weights(h, weights, n, N):
    center=n-1
    w0=float(weights[center])
    b=np.full(n, w0, dtype=float)
    if n > 1:
        w_neg = weights[:center][::-1]
        w_pos = weights[center+1:]
        cp = np.cumsum(w_pos)
        b[1:] += cp
        cn = np.cumsum(w_neg)
        b[:center] += cn[::-1]
        S = w_pos + w_neg
        K = np.zeros(N, dtype=float)
        H = np.zeros(N, dtype=float)
        H[:n] = h
        K[0] = 2.0*w0
        d = np.arange(1, n)
        K[d] += S
        K[N-d] += S
        y = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(K,N) * np.conj(np.fft.rfft(H,N)), N).real
        idx = (N - np.arange(n)) % N
        prod = y[idx]
    else:
        prod = 2.0*w0*h
    return b - prod
```
Need if n=1, `weights` length1, `b` length1, prod array length1. Good.

`lse_gradient`:
```
def lse_gradient(x):
    n=_N; c=compute_c_all(x,n,_NFFT)
    m=float(np.max(c)); a=_ALPHA
    sh=a*(c-m); maxsh=float(np.max(sh)); p=np.exp(sh-maxsh); S=float(p.sum())
    if S<=0 or not np.isfinite(S):
       return np.zeros(n)
    w = p / (a * S)
    raw = raw_gradient_from_weights(x, w, n, _NFFT)
    return raw * (2.0/n)
```
If c max NaN? p NaN; return zeros? Maybe. Check `if not np.isfinite(m): return np.zeros(n)`.

`project_feasible`: allocate h array. Ensure target if n None.
```
def project_feasible(h, n=None):
    h=np.asarray(h,dtype=float)
    if n is None: n=len(h)
    target=float(n)/2.0
    h=np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    if n == 0: return h
    lo=float(np.min(h)) - 1.0
    hi=float(np.max(h)) + 1.0
    for _ in range(70):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       s=float(np.clip(h-mid,0.0,1.0).sum())
       if s > target:
          lo=mid
       else:
          hi=mid
    tau=0.5*(lo+hi)
    hp=np.clip(h-tau,0.0,1.0)
    for _ in range(20):
       diff=target - float(hp.sum())
       if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
       free = (hp > 1e-12) & (hp < 1.0 - 1e-12)
       if free.any():
          hp[free] += diff / float(np.count_nonzero(free))
       else:
          # if diff positive, increase zeros; if negative decrease ones
          if diff > 0:
             zeros = (hp <= 1e-12)
             if zeros.any(): hp[zeros] += diff / float(np.count_nonzero(zeros))
          else:
             ones = (hp >= 1.0 - 1e-12)
             if ones.any(): hp[ones] -= -diff / float(np.count_nonzero(ones))
    return np.clip(hp,0.0,1.0)
```
If diff nonfree and no zeros/ones (all at bounds) impossible unless diff due target unreachable; ignore.

`exact_c(h,n)`:
```
def exact_c(h,n):
   c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
   val=float(np.max(c))*(2.0/n)
   return val if np.isfinite(val) else 1e100
```

`lbfgsb_step` and `optimize_lbfgsb`:
```
def lbfgsb_step(h,n,alpha,maxiter,rho):
    set_ll_state(n,alpha,rho)
    h0=project_feasible(h,n)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-12,'maxls':20,'disp':False}
    try:
       res=minimize(ll_objective,h0,method='L-BFGS-B',bounds=bounds,jac=ll_gradient,options=options)
       return project_feasible(res.x,n)
    except Exception:
       return h0
```

def optimize_lbfgsb(h_start,n,deadline,alphas,maxiter):
    h_best=project_feasible(h_start,n).copy()
    c_best=fast_max_c(h_best)
    for alpha in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        if alpha <= 0: continue
        # maybe two rho: [20,100] if n <=300 and time left? Use one 50.
        for rho in (20.0, 80.0):
            if time.time() > deadline: break
            h1=lbfgsb_step(h_best,n,alpha,maxiter,int(rho? no, maxiter))
            c1=fast_max_c(h1)
            if c1 < c_best:
                c_best=c1; h_best=h1.copy()
    return h_best,c_best
```

`apply_binary_flip` as above. Need ensure `delta` float but values integer. Use `if delta >0`? It only -1/+1.
```
def apply_binary_flip(c,h,p,delta):
    n=len(h); center=n-1
    prod=np.zeros(n,dtype=float)
    if p > 0:
        prod[1:p+1] += h[0:p][::-1]
    if p < n-1:
        prod[1:n-p] += h[p+1:n]
    prod[1:] *= -delta
    if center+1 < 2*n-1:
        c[center+1:] += prod[1:]
        c[:center][::-1] += prod[1:]
    qp = p if p < n else n-1
    if qp > 0:
        c[center+1:center+1+qp] += delta
    qn = n-1-p
    if qn > 0:
        c[:center][::-1][:qn] += delta
    h[p] += delta
```
For n=1, center0, no positives. `prod` length1. But binary n>=2.
Potential issue: `c[:center][::-1][:qn]` is a view of reversed first center elements; first qn corresponds d=1..qn. Good.

But note: adding product to negative lags after adding to positive uses modified c? No, product same. Good.

`binary_local_search`:
```
def binary_local_search(h_cont,n,deadline,max_sweeps=10):
    if n <= 0 or (n % 2 !=0):
       return h_cont, fast_max_c(h_cont)
    target=n//2
    h=np.zeros(n)
    order=np.argsort(h_cont)[::-1]
    h[order[:target]]=1.0
    N=nfft_for(n)
    c=np.rint(compute_c_all(h,n,N))
    center=n-1; c[center]=0.0
    best_c=float(c.max())
    best_h=h.copy()
    ones=[i for i in range(n) if h[i]==1.0]
    zeros=[i for i in range(n) if h[i]==0.0]
    for sweep in range(max_sweeps):
       if time.time() > deadline: break
       # recompute to sanitize
       c = np.rint(compute_c_all(h,n,N)); c[center]=0.0
       curbest = float(c.max())
       if curbest < best_c:
          best_c=curbest; best_h=h.copy()
       improved=False
       # randomize order? deterministic okay. To avoid bias, maybe alternate based on sweep.
       for i in ones:
          if time.time() > deadline: break
          for j in zeros:
             if time.time() > deadline: break
             # current checks h[i]==1, h[j]==0 (lists may stale if rebuild? we rebuild each sweep and after accept break)
             tmp=c.copy()
             apply_binary_flip(tmp,h,i,-1.0)
             apply_binary_flip(tmp,h,j,1.0)
             tmp=np.rint(tmp)
             nb=float(tmp.max())
             # revert
             h[i]=1.0; h[j]=0.0
             if nb + 1e-9 < best_c:
                c=tmp
                h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
                best_c=nb
                best_h=h.copy()
                ones=[k for k in range(n) if h[k]==1.0]
                zeros=[k for k in range(n) if h[k]==0.0]
                improved=True
                break
          if improved or time.time()>deadline: break
       if not improved: break
    return best_h, best_c*(2.0/n)
```
Potential when accepted, after applying flips h is new. We then set best_h copy, rebuild lists. We do not need revert. But in code after revert line, we set h[i]=0,h[j]=1 after we already set h[i]=1,h[j]=0 for revert. That works. However we did `apply` twice, so h currently new. Then `h[i]=1; h[j]=0` reverts for compare. If accept, we set h[i]=0,h[j]=1. Good.

If not accept and time out, we revert before break. Good.

If improved and we break inner, h is new; we rebuild. Good.

But if time check inside inner after revert? We check before candidate. If time expires during candidate, after candidate revert and if improve maybe set; fine.

Let's write run carefully with time variables.

```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time()
    deadline = t0 + max(60.0, budget_s) - 8.0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        parent = np.full(250, 0.5)
    if parent.ndim != 1:
        parent = parent.ravel()
    if parent.size < 50:
        parent = np.full(250, 0.5)
    n_par = int(parent.size)
    h_best = project_feasible(parent, n_par)
    c_best = fast_max_c(h_best)
    n_best = n_par
    # sym
    hs = project_feasible(0.5*(h_best + h_best[::-1]), n_best)
    cs = fast_max_c(hs)
    if cs < c_best:
        h_best = hs; c_best = cs
    total = max(60.0, budget_s) - 5.0
    core_end = t0 + 0.42*total
    # parent lbfgs
    if n_best <= 350:
        alphas_parent = [5.0, 10.0, 20.0, 50.0, 100.0, 200.0, 500.0, 1000.0, 2000.0]
        maxiter_parent = 220
    elif n_best <= 600:
        alphas_parent = [10.0, 30.0, 100.0, 300.0, 1000.0]
        maxiter_parent = 120
    else:
        alphas_parent = [10.0, 100.0, 500.0]
        maxiter_parent = 80
    h2,c2 = optimize_lbfgsb(h_best, n_best, core_end, alphas_parent, maxiter_parent, [20.0])
    if c2 < c_best:
       h_best=h2; c_best=c2; n_best=n_best # same
    # parent slsqp
    if n_best <= 300 and time.time() < core_end - 100:
        h3,c3=optimize_slsqp(h_best,n_best,core_end,[200.0,500.0,1000.0],120)
        if c3<c_best: update
    # noise start
    if time.time() < core_end - 60 and n_best <= 500:
        h_no = project_feasible(h_best + 0.08*rng.randn(n_best), n_best)
        h4,c4=optimize_lbfgsb(h_no,n_best,core_end,[50.0,200.0,500.0],100,[20.0])
        if c4<c_best: update
    res_end = t0 + 0.78*total
    done_ns = set()
    done_ns.add(n_best)
    candidates = [200, 300, 400, 250, 350, 500, 600, 750]
    for nn in candidates:
        if nn in done_ns or nn %2 !=0 or nn < 100: continue
        if time.time() > res_end - 60: break
        done_ns.add(nn)
        h = resample_h(h_best, nn)
        hs2 = project_feasible(0.5*(h+h[::-1]), nn)
        if fast_max_c(hs2) < fast_max_c(h): h=hs2
        if nn <= 300:
            alphas_nn=[10.0,50.0,200.0,500.0,1000.0]; maxit_nn=120
        elif nn <= 500:
            alphas_nn=[20.0,100.0,300.0,1000.0]; maxit_nn=80
        else:
            alphas_nn=[20.0,100.0,300.0]; maxit_nn=50
        h_new,c_new=optimize_lbfgsb(h,nn,min(res_end, time.time()+180), alphas_nn, maxit_nn, [20.0])
        # note min(res_end, time+180) prevents one n using all res_end? But a call could exceed 180; okay.
        if c_new < c_best:
            h_best=h_new; c_best=c_new; n_best=nn
        if nn <= 300 and time.time() < res_end - 80:
            h_sl,c_sl=optimize_slsqp(h_best,nn,min(res_end, time.time()+80),[500.0,1000.0],80)
            if c_sl < c_best: update
    # sym after all? maybe
    if time.time() < deadline - 40:
        hs3 = project_feasible(0.5*(h_best+h_best[::-1]), n_best)
        cs3=fast_max_c(hs3)
        if cs3 < c_best:
            h_best=hs3; c_best=cs3
    # active polish
    if time.time() < deadline - 45:
        h_act,c_act=active_max_polish(h_best,n_best,deadline-15, max_iter=4000)
        if c_act < c_best: update
    # binary
    bin_ns = [n_best]
    if n_best != 250:
       bin_ns.append(250)
    for bn in bin_ns:
        if (bn % 2 !=0) or bn > 400: continue
        if time.time() < deadline - 25:
            h_bin = resample_h(h_best, bn) if bn != n_best else h_best
            hb, cb = binary_local_search(h_bin, bn, deadline-5, max_sweeps=6 if bn <= 250 else 3)
            if cb < c_best:
                h_best=hb; c_best=cb; n_best=bn
    # final exact maybe sym? project
    h_best=project_feasible(h_best,n_best)
    c_exact=exact_c(h_best,n_best)
    # If exact worse than tracked due numerical, could use tracked h. But exact is judge.
    # Do one more small active polish at final if exact > c_best? Not know.
    return h_best, float(c_exact), int(n_best)
```

- If y-τ >=1 (τ ≤ y-1): contribution 1.
- If 0< y-τ <1 (y-1 < τ < y): contribution y-τ.
- If τ ≥ y: contribution 0.

```python
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize

_N = 0
_ALPHA = 1.0
_NFFT = 0
_TARGET = 0.0
_RHO = 20.0


def nfft_for(n):
    N = 1
    need = 2 * int(n)
    if need < 2:
        need = 2
    while N < need:
        N <<= 1
    return N


def compute_c_all(h, n, N):
    if n == 1:
        H = np.zeros(N)
        H[0] = h[0]
        F = np.fft.rfft(H, N)
        R = np.fft.irfft(F * np.conj(F), N).real
        total = float(h[0])
        return np.array([total - R[0]], dtype=float)

    H = np.zeros(N, dtype=float)
    H[:n] = h
    F = np.fft.rfft(H, N)
    R = np.fft.irfft(F * np.conj(F), N).real
    P = R[:n]

    prefix = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=float)
    prefix[0] = 0.0
    prefix[1:] = np.cumsum(h)
    total = prefix[n]

    pos = total - prefix[:n] - P
    neg = prefix[n:0:-1] - P

    c = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    center = n - 1
    c[:center] = neg[1:][::-1]
    c[center] = pos[0]
    c[center + 1:] = pos[1:]
    return c


def fast_max_c(h):
    n = len(h)
    N = nfft_for(n)
    c = compute_c_all(h, n, N)
    val = float(np.max(c)) * (2.0 / n)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return 1e100
    return val


def exact_c(h, n):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    val = float(np.max(c)) * (2.0 / n)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return 1e100
    return val


def set_lse_state(n, alpha):
    global _N, _ALPHA, _NFFT, _TARGET
    _N = int(n)
    _ALPHA = float(alpha)
    _TARGET = 0.5 * float(_N)
    _NFFT = nfft_for(_N)


def set_ll_state(n, alpha, rho):
    global _N, _ALPHA, _NFFT, _TARGET, _RHO
    _N = int(n)
    _ALPHA = float(alpha)
    _TARGET = 0.5 * float(_N)
    _NFFT = nfft_for(_N)
    _RHO = float(rho)


def lse_objective(x):
    n = _N
    c = compute_c_all(x, n, _NFFT)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return 1e100
    a = _ALPHA
    sh = a * (c - m)
    maxsh = float(np.max(sh))
    p = np.exp(sh - maxsh)
    S = float(p.sum())
    if S <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(S):
        return 1e100
    val = (m + (maxsh + np.log(S)) / a) * (2.0 / n)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return 1e100
    return val


def raw_gradient_from_weights(h, weights, n, N):
    center = n - 1
    w0 = float(weights[center])
    b = np.full(n, w0, dtype=float)

    if n > 1:
        w_neg = weights[:center][::-1]
        w_pos = weights[center + 1:]

        cp = np.cumsum(w_pos)
        b[1:] += cp

        cn = np.cumsum(w_neg)
        b[:center] += cn[::-1]

        S = w_pos + w_neg
        K = np.zeros(N, dtype=float)
        H = np.zeros(N, dtype=float)
        H[:n] = h

        K[0] = 2.0 * w0
        if n > 1:
            d = np.arange(1, n)
            K[d] += S
            K[N - d] += S

        y = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(K, N) * np.conj(np.fft.rfft(H, N)), N).real
        idx = (N - np.arange(n)) % N
        prod = y[idx]
    else:
        prod = 2.0 * w0 * h

    return b - prod


def lse_gradient(x):
    n = _N
    c = compute_c_all(x, n, _NFFT)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=float)

    a = _ALPHA
    sh = a * (c - m)
    maxsh = float(np.max(sh))
    p = np.exp(sh - maxsh)
    S = float(p.sum())
    if S <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(S):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=float)

    w = p / (a * S)
    raw = raw_gradient_from_weights(x, w, n, _NFFT)
    return raw * (2.0 / n)


def constraint_fun(x):
    return float(np.sum(x) - _TARGET)


def constraint_jac(x):
    return np.ones_like(x, dtype=float)


def ll_objective(x):
    f = lse_objective(x)
    e = float(np.sum(x) - _TARGET)
    return f + 0.5 * _RHO * e * e


def ll_gradient(x):
    g = lse_gradient(x)
    e = float(np.sum(x) - _TARGET)
    return g + _RHO * e


def project_feasible(h, n=None):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if n is None:
        n = int(len(h))
    if n <= 0:
        return h

    target = 0.5 * float(n)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
        h = np.full(n, 0.5, dtype=float)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)

    lo = float(np.min(h)) - 1.0
    hi = float(np.max(h)) + 1.0

    for _ in range(46):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = float(np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum())
        if s > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    tau = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
    hp = np.clip(h - tau, 0.0, 1.0)

    for _ in range(12):
        diff = target - float(hp.sum())
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13:
            break
        free = (hp > 1e-12) & (hp < 1.0 - 1e-12)
        if free.any():
            hp[free] += diff / float(np.count_nonzero(free))
        else:
            if diff > 0.0:
                z = hp <= 1e-12
                if z.any():
                    hp[z] += diff / float(np.count_nonzero(z))
            else:
                o = hp >= 1.0 - 1e-12
                if o.any():
                    hp[o] += diff / float(np.count_nonzero(o))

    return np.clip(hp, 0.0, 1.0)


def resample_h(h, n):
    m = int(len(h))
    n = int(n)
    if m == n:
        return np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    x0 = (np.arange(m) + 0.5) * (2.0 / m)
    x1 = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / n)
    return np.interp(x1, x0, h)


def lbfgsb_step(h, n, alpha, maxiter, rho):
    set_ll_state(n, alpha, rho)
    h0 = project_feasible(h, n)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    options = {
        "maxiter": int(maxiter),
        "ftol": 1e-12,
        "maxls": 20,
        "disp": False,
    }
    try:
        res = minimize(
            ll_objective,
            h0,
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            bounds=bounds,
            jac=ll_gradient,
            options=options,
        )
        return project_feasible(res.x, n)
    except Exception:
        return h0


def optimize_lbfgsb(h_start, n, deadline, alphas, maxiter, rhos):
    n = int(n)
    h_best = project_feasible(h_start, n).copy()
    c_best = fast_max_c(h_best)
    h_work = h_best.copy()

    for alpha in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        if not np.isfinite(alpha) or alpha <= 0.0:
            continue
        for rho in rhos:
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break
            h1 = lbfgsb_step(h_work, n, alpha, maxiter, rho)
            c1 = fast_max_c(h1)
            if c1 < c_best:
                c_best = c1
                h_best = h1.copy()
                h_work = h1.copy()
            elif c1 <= c_best + 1e-8:
                h_work = h1.copy()

    return h_best, c_best


def slsqp_step(h, n, alpha, maxiter):
    set_lse_state(n, alpha)
    n = int(n)
    h0 = project_feasible(h, n)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    cons = [
        {
            "type": "eq",
            "fun": constraint_fun,
            "jac": constraint_jac,
        }
    ]
    options = {
        "maxiter": int(maxiter),
        "ftol": 1e-12,
        "disp": False,
    }
    try:
        res = minimize(
            lse_objective,
            h0,
            method="SLSQP",
            bounds=bounds,
            constraints=cons,
            jac=lse_gradient,
            options=options,
        )
        h1 = project_feasible(res.x, n)
        return h1, fast_max_c(h1)
    except Exception:
        return h0, fast_max_c(h0)


def optimize_slsqp(h_start, n, deadline, alphas, maxiter):
    n = int(n)
    h_best = project_feasible(h_start, n).copy()
    c_best = fast_max_c(h_best)
    h_work = h_best.copy()

    for alpha in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        if not np.isfinite(alpha) or alpha <= 0.0:
            continue
        h1, c1 = slsqp_step(h_work, n, alpha, maxiter)
        if c1 < c_best:
            c_best = c1
            h_best = h1.copy()
            h_work = h1.copy()
        elif c1 <= c_best + 1e-8:
            h_work = h1.copy()

    return h_best, c_best


def active_max_polish(h, n, deadline, max_iter=1200):
    n = int(n)
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = fast_max_c(h)
    N = nfft_for(n)
    dx = 2.0 / n
    lr = 0.04
    curr_c = best_c

    for _ in range(int(max_iter)):
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break

        c = compute_c_all(h, n, N)
        m = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(m):
            break
        curr_c = m * dx

        if curr_c < best_c:
            best_c = curr_c
            best_h = h.copy()

        tol = 1e-5
        active = np.where(c >= m - tol)[0]
        if active.size == 0:
            active = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)

        w = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
        w[active] = 1.0 / float(active.size)

        raw = raw_gradient_from_weights(h, w, n, N)
        g = dx * raw
        g = g - np.mean(g)
        ng = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
        if ng < 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(ng):
            break

        direction = g / ng
        found = False

        for factor in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.03, 0.01):
            step = lr * factor
            ht = project_feasible(h - step * direction, n)
            ct = fast_max_c(ht)
            if ct < curr_c - 1e-14:
                h = ht
                curr_c = ct
                if ct < best_c:
                    best_c = ct
                    best_h = h.copy()
                found = True
                break

        if found:
            lr = min(lr * 1.04, 1.0)
        else:
            lr *= 0.78
            if lr < 1e-10:
                break

    return best_h, best_c


def apply_binary_flip(c, h, p, delta):
    n = len(h)
    center = n - 1
    prod = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)

    if p > 0:
        prod[1:p + 1] += h[0:p][::-1]
    if p < n - 1:
        prod[1:n - p] += h[p + 1:n]

    prod[1:] *= -delta

    if center + 1 < 2 * n - 1:
        c[center + 1:] += prod[1:]
        c[:center][::-1] += prod[1:]

    qp = min(p, n - 1)
    if qp > 0:
        c[center + 1:center + 1 + qp] += delta

    qn = n - 1 - p
    if qn > 0:
        c[:center][::-1][:qn] += delta

    h[p] += delta


def binary_local_search(h_cont, n, deadline, max_sweeps=6):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0 or n % 2 != 0:
        return h_cont, fast_max_c(h_cont)

    target = n // 2
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    order = np.argsort(h_cont)[::-1]
    h[order[:target]] = 1.0

    N = nfft_for(n)
    center = n - 1
    c = np.rint(compute_c_all(h, n, N))
    c[center] = 0.0

    best_c = float(c.max())
    best_h = h.copy()
    ones = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] == 1.0]
    zeros = [i for i in range(n) if h[i] == 0.0]

    for _sweep in range(int(max_sweeps)):
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break

        c = np.rint(compute_c_all(h, n, N))
        c[center] = 0.0
        cur_best = float(c.max())
        if cur_best < best_c:
            best_c = cur_best
            best_h = h.copy()

        improved = False
        for i in ones:
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break
            for j in zeros:
                if time.time() > deadline:
                    break

                tmp = c.copy()
                apply_binary_flip(tmp, h, i, -1.0)
                apply_binary_flip(tmp, h, j, 1.0)
                tmp = np.rint(tmp)
                nb = float(tmp.max())

                h[i] = 1.0
                h[j] = 0.0

                if nb + 1e-9 < best_c:
                    c = tmp
                    h[i] = 0.0
                    h[j] = 1.0
                    best_c = nb
                    best_h = h.copy()
                    ones = [k for k in range(n) if h[k] == 1.0]
                    zeros = [k for k in range(n) if h[k] == 0.0]
                    improved = True
                    break

            if improved or time.time() > deadline:
                break

        if not improved:
            break

    return best_h, best_c * (2.0 / n)


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    budget = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t0 + max(60.0, budget) - 8.0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(int(seed))

    try:
        parent = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        parent = np.full(250, 0.5, dtype=float)

    if parent.ndim != 1:
        parent = parent.ravel()
    if parent.size < 50:
        parent = np.full(250, 0.5, dtype=float)

    n_parent = int(parent.size)
    n_best = n_parent
    h_best = project_feasible(parent, n_parent)
    c_best = fast_max_c(h_best)

    hs = project_feasible(0.5 * (h_best + h_best[::-1]), n_best)
    cs = fast_max_c(hs)
    if cs < c_best:
        h_best = hs
        c_best = cs

    total = max(60.0, budget) - 5.0
    core_end = t0 + 0.42 * total

    if n_best <= 350:
        alphas_parent = [5.0, 10.0, 20.0, 50.0, 100.0, 200.0, 500.0, 1000.0, 2000.0, 5000.0]
        maxiter_parent = 220
    elif n_best <= 600:
        alphas_parent = [10.0, 30.0, 100.0, 300.0, 1000.0, 2000.0]
        maxiter_parent = 130
    else:
        alphas_parent = [10.0, 100.0, 500.0, 1000.0]
        maxiter_parent = 80

    h_alf, c_alf = optimize_lbfgsb(
        h_best,
        n_best,
        core_end,
        alphas_parent,
        maxiter_parent,
        [20.0],
    )
    if c_alf < c_best:
        h_best = h_alf
        c_best = c_alf

    if n_best <= 300 and time.time() < core_end - 120.0:
        h_sl, c_sl = optimize_slsqp(
            h_best,
            n_best,
            core_end,
            [200.0, 500.0, 1000.0, 2000.0],
            110,
        )
        if c_sl < c_best:
            h_best = h_sl
            c_best = c_sl

    if time.time() < core_end - 70.0 and n_best <= 500:
        h_noise = project_feasible(h_best + 0.08 * rng.randn(n_best), n_best)
        h_no, c_no = optimize_lbfgsb(
            h_noise,
            n_best,
            core_end,
            [50.0, 200.0, 500.0, 1000.0],
            100,
            [20.0],
        )
        if c_no < c_best:
            h_best = h_no
            c_best = c_no

    res_end = t0 + 0.78 * total
    other_ns = [200, 300, 400, 500]
    done_ns = {n_best}

    for nn in other_ns:
        nn = int(nn)
        if nn in done_ns:
            continue
        if nn % 2 != 0 or nn < 100:
            continue
        if time.time() > res_end - 90.0:
            break
        done_ns.add(nn)

        h = resample_h(h_best, nn)
        hs_n = project_feasible(0.5 * (h + h[::-1]), nn)
        if fast_max_c(hs_n) < fast_max_c(h):
            h = hs_n

        if nn <= 300:
            alphas_nn = [10.0, 50.0, 200.0, 500.0, 1000.0]
            maxit_nn = 120
        elif nn <= 500:
            alphas_nn = [20.0, 100.0, 300.0, 1000.0]
            maxit_nn = 80
        else:
            alphas_nn = [20.0, 100.0, 300.0]
            maxit_nn = 50

        local_end = min(res_end, time.time() + 120.0)
        h_new, c_new = optimize_lbfgsb(h, nn, local_end, alphas_nn, maxit_nn, [20.0])
        if c_new < c_best:
            h_best = h_new
            c_best = c_new
            n_best = nn

        if nn <= 300 and time.time() < res_end - 110.0:
            local_end2 = min(res_end, time.time() + 70.0)
            h_sln, c_sln = optimize_slsqp(h_best, nn, local_end2, [500.0, 1000.0], 80)
            if c_sln < c_best:
                h_best = h_sln
                c_best = c_sln
                n_best = nn

    if time.time() < deadline - 45.0:
        h_sym = project_feasible(0.5 * (h_best + h_best[::-1]), n_best)
        c_sym = fast_max_c(h_sym)
        if c_sym < c_best:
            h_best = h_sym
            c_best = c_sym

    if time.time() < deadline - 50.0:
        h_act, c_act = active_max_polish(h_best, n_best, deadline - 18.0, max_iter=900)
        if c_act < c_best:
            h_best = h_act
            c_best = c_act

    bin_ns = [n_best]
    if n_best != 250:
        bin_ns.append(250)

    for bn in bin_ns:
        bn = int(bn)
        if bn % 2 != 0 or bn > 400:
            continue
        if time.time() >= deadline - 25.0:
            break
        if bn == n_best:
            h_bin = np.asarray(h_best, dtype=float).copy()
        else:
            h_bin = resample_h(h_best, bn)
        sweeps = 6 if bn <= 250 else 3
        hb, cb = binary_local_search(h_bin, bn, deadline - 4.0, max_sweeps=sweeps)
        if cb < c_best:
            h_best = hb
            c_best = cb
            n_best = bn

    h_best = project_feasible(h_best, int(n_best))
    c_final = exact_c(h_best, int(n_best))
    return h_best, float(c_final), int(n_best)
```
